2026-10-17T17:23:25.0763688Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [require-build] [cluster:firmus] [nodes:2] [profile] Balance Prefill MegaMoE EP ranks with a static expert permutation

One factor against reference run 38945855851 (6521.98 good output tok/s,
c192, P3/D1, every worker TP4/DP4/EP4, MegaMoE on both roles, EAGLE3
steps 3 / draft 4, image e29dfde4...): the Prefill workers load a static,
precision-neutral placement of the 128 experts over their 4 EP ranks.
Concurrency, caps, dispatch, speculation and Decode are unchanged; Decode
keeps the trivial placement. The source change is a no-op for trivial
placement, so this image's other behaviour equals main's.

Why: the Prefill MegaMoE kernel averages 2.17 ms/layer in serving but the
balanced-routing gate measures 1.66 ms at 4096 tokens/rank. Statistics
run 39051152456 (commit aef6cf6, SGLang stat recorder on the three
Prefill workers over the whole official client run, 1640 MoE passes,
25.7 M tokens) shows that real routing is strongly skewed: per layer the
hottest expert gets 7.7-16x the mean share and the top 8 experts take
52% of assignments on average. With the trivial layout (rank r owns
experts 32r..32r+31) the slowest rank carries a token-weighted per-pass
max/mean of 1.338 averaged over the 94 layers (median 1.31, worst layer
86 at 2.21). Because the fused kernel waits for the slowest rank,
1.66 ms x (1.338 - 1.034) predicts ~0.50 ms/layer, which matches the
missing serving time.

Placement: SGLang's EPLB balanced packing (greedy LPT, exactly 32 experts
per rank, zero redundant experts) applied per layer to the aggregate
logical counts of all three workers; reimplemented in numpy because the
recorder dump was analysed off-GPU. In sample the per-pass max/mean
falls to 1.034 (p90 1.044, worst 1.054); placements fitted on two
workers and scored on the third, or fitted on the first half of the
passes and scored on the second, give 1.034-1.035, so the skew is a
property of the model and workload, not noise. The file is
configs/firmus-prefill-expert-location.json in SGLang's
init_by_mapping format ({"physical_to_logical_map": 94 x 128}).

Source change (patches/sglang/qwen3-sm90-megamoe.patch): the MegaMoE
branch of Qwen3 MoE called self.topk without expert_location_dispatch_info
and passed logical ids to the kernel, while FusedMoE's weight loader
already places weights by physical id. With a placement file that would
have been silently wrong. The branch now passes
ExpertLocationDispatchInfo.init_new(layer_id) exactly like the DeepEP
branch; SGLang returns None when ep_dispatch_algorithm is unset (trivial
placement), and with a placement file server_args resolves it to
"static" (a2a backend is not "none"), whose remap is a per-layer
128-entry gather inside the already-compiled topk post-process. The
kernel treats physical ids 32r..32r+31 as rank r's experts, matching
SGLang's layout. The adapter guard still forbids EPLB rebalancing and
redundant experts. The full patch chain applies to the pinned source and
check-firmus-megamoe-source.py now asserts the dispatch info is passed.

Runtime wiring: prefill.expert_location in the config ("trivial" or a
repository path); provisioning copies the file to
source/prefill-expert-location.json and the Prefill workers get
--init-expert-location; check-firmus-server-info.py requires that path
and ep_dispatch_algorithm=static on every Prefill DP state and trivial /
None on Decode. Because the official parser does not judge output
content, the job also stores one greedy chat completion
(results/sanity-chat.json) to show the permuted Prefill still produces
coherent text; EAGLE3 acceptance on Decode is a second check.

Checks after the run: MegaMoE gate PASS, server-info static placement,
"init_expert_location from init_by_mapping" in the Prefill logs,
coherent sanity answer, acceptance length unchanged, official validation
valid, and the Prefill MegaMoE kernel mean from the profile versus
2.17 ms. Shell syntax, the line-continuation check, ruff and the full
test suite pass locally.
